## GKA-001 · L1 · easy · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · foundation

The Arthashastra, a treatise on statecraft and economic policy, is traditionally attributed to:

- **A.** Kalidasa  _(error: author of Abhijnanashakuntalam, not a political treatise)_
- **B.** Vishakhadatta  _(error: wrote the play Mudrarakshasa about Chanakya)_
- **C.** Kautilya ✅
- **D.** Banabhatta  _(error: author of Harshacharita)_

**Working**

1. The Arthashastra is attributed to Kautilya (Chanakya/Vishnugupta), minister of Chandragupta Maurya.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Vishakhadatta wrote ABOUT Chanakya (Mudrarakshasa); he did not write the Arthashastra.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-002 · L1 · easy · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · foundation

The Sanskrit play Mudrarakshasa, which depicts the rise of Chandragupta Maurya, was written by:

- **A.** Vishakhadatta ✅
- **B.** Kalidasa  _(error: Gupta-age poet-dramatist)_
- **C.** Bhasa  _(error: early dramatist, Svapnavasavadatta)_
- **D.** Sudraka  _(error: author of Mrichchhakatika)_

**Working**

1. Mudrarakshasa ('The Signet Ring of Rakshasa') is Vishakhadatta's political drama on Chanakya and Chandragupta.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Do not confuse with Sudraka's Mrichchhakatika (The Little Clay Cart).

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-003 · L1 · easy · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · foundation

'Indica', an account of Mauryan India, was written by:

- **A.** Hiuen Tsang  _(error: Chinese pilgrim in Harsha's time)_
- **B.** Fa-Hien  _(error: Chinese pilgrim in the Gupta period)_
- **C.** Megasthenes ✅
- **D.** Ptolemy  _(error: Greek geographer, 2nd century CE)_

**Working**

1. Megasthenes, ambassador of Seleucus Nicator at Chandragupta Maurya's court in Pataliputra, wrote Indica (surviving in fragments).

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Chinese pilgrims wrote travel records (Fo-kuo-ki, Si-yu-ki), not Indica.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-004 · L1 · easy · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · foundation

Harshacharita, a biography of King Harshavardhana, was composed by his court poet:

- **A.** Harshavardhana himself  _(error: Harsha wrote plays such as Ratnavali, not his biography)_

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Harsha authored plays (Ratnavali, Nagananda, Priyadarshika) but not his own biography.

## GKA-006 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about the Arthashastra:

1. Its Saptanga theory describes the state as having seven elements, including Swami, Amatya and Kosha.
2. A manuscript of the text was found and published by R. Shamasastry in the early twentieth century.
3. It was composed mainly in Pali.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 2 only ✅
- **C.** 2 only  _(error: rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 2 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 3; rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: the seven limbs are Swami, Amatya, Janapada, Durga, Kosha, Danda and Mitra
2. Statement 2 is correct: Shamasastry identified the manuscript in 1905 and published it in 1909
3. Statement 3 is incorrect: it is written in Sanskrit
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 2 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Pali is the language of the Buddhist canon, not of the Arthashastra.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-007 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Match List I (Text) with List II (Author):

| List I | List II |
|---|---|
| A. Ashtadhyayi | 1. Kamandaka |
| B. Mahabhashya | 2. Patanjali |
| C. Nitisara | 3. Panini |
| D. Mrichchhakatika | 4. Sudraka |

Select the correct answer using the codes below:

- **A.** A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4 ✅
- **B.** A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  _(error: mismatches A, C, D)_
- **C.** A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  _(error: mismatches B, C, D)_
- **D.** A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  _(error: mismatches A, B, C)_

**Working**

1. Ashtadhyayi → Panini (Sanskrit grammar)
2. Mahabhashya → Patanjali (commentary on Panini)
3. Nitisara → Kamandaka (treatise on polity)
4. Mrichchhakatika → Sudraka (drama)
5. Code: A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Patanjali's Mahabhashya is a commentary on Panini's grammar — keep the two grammarians distinct.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-009 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): Ashoka's Dhamma was not a new religion.

Reason (R): It stressed ethical conduct — tolerance, non-violence, respect for elders and humane treatment of servants — meant for people of all sects.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **B.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A ✅
- **C.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **D.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_

**Working**

1. Ashoka's edicts present Dhamma as a moral code for all subjects; its universal ethical content is why it is not treated as a separate religion.
2. A is true; R is true; R does explain A.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Ashoka personally followed Buddhism, but Dhamma itself was a general ethical code.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-010 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Arrange the following in chronological order:

1. Fa-Hien's travels in India
2. Hiuen Tsang's travels in India
3. Fourth Buddhist Council under Kanishka
4. Megasthenes at the court of Pataliputra

Select the correct chronological order (earliest first):

- **A.** 4 – 1 – 2 – 3  _(error: misplaces 3, 1, 2 in the sequence)_
- **B.** 4 – 3 – 1 – 2 ✅
- **C.** 4 – 2 – 3 – 1  _(error: misplaces 3, 1, 2 in the sequence)_
- **D.** 1 – 3 – 2 – 4  _(error: misplaces 4, 1, 2 in the sequence)_

**Working**

1. Megasthenes at the court of Pataliputra — c. 300 BCE (Chandragupta Maurya)
2. Fourth Buddhist Council under Kanishka — c. 1st–2nd century CE
3. Fa-Hien's travels in India — c. 399–412 CE
4. Hiuen Tsang's travels in India — c. 629–645 CE
5. Order: 4 – 3 – 1 – 2.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** The Kushana council precedes both Chinese pilgrims; Fa-Hien (Gupta) precedes Hiuen Tsang (Harsha).

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-011 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about early Tamil (Sangam and post-Sangam) literature:

1. Tolkappiyam is a work on Tamil grammar and poetics.
2. Silappadikaram was composed by Ilango Adigal.
3. Manimekalai was composed by Kamban.

Which of the statements given above is/are NOT correct?

- **A.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 1, 2 (correct statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 1 (correct statements misjudged))_
- **C.** 2 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 2 (correct statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 3 only ✅

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: it is the oldest extant Tamil grammar
2. Statement 2 is correct: the epic of Kannagi and Kovalan
3. Statement 3 is incorrect: Manimekalai is by Sittalai Sattanar; Kamban wrote the Tamil Ramayana much later
4. Hence the answer is: 3 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Kamban (Ramavataram) belongs to the Chola period, not to the twin epics.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-012 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Consider the following pairs of Mauryan officials (as described in the Arthashastra) and their functions:

1. Samaharta — chief collector of revenue
2. Sannidhata — chief of the treasury and stores
3. Sitadhyaksha — superintendent of crown agriculture
4. Akshapataladhyaksha — superintendent of elephants

How many of the pairs given above are correctly matched?

- **A.** Only three ✅
- **B.** All four  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **C.** Only one  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **D.** Only two  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_

**Working**

1. Pair 1 is correct: Samaharta supervised revenue collection
2. Pair 2 is correct: Sannidhata was the treasurer/custodian of stores
3. Pair 3 is correct: Sita = crown lands; Sitadhyaksha supervised agriculture on them
4. Pair 4 is incorrect: Akshapatala was the accounts and records office; elephants were under the Hastyadhyaksha
5. 3 of 4 are correct — Only three.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** The 'aksha' prefix misleads — Akshapatala is the accounts office.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-013 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): In Kautilya's Mandala theory, the kingdom lying beyond the immediate neighbour is regarded as a natural ally.

Reason (R): The immediate neighbour is treated as the natural enemy (ari), and the enemy's enemy is treated as a friend.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **B.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **C.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **D.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A ✅

**Working**

1. The Mandala is a circle of states: the adjoining state is the ari; the state beyond it (the mitra) shares that enemy and so is a natural ally.
2. A is true; R is true; R does explain A.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** R supplies the logic (enemy's enemy is a friend) that makes A follow.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-014 · L3 · hard · Ancient Indian texts and statecraft ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about works of the Gupta age:

1. Amarakosha is a Sanskrit lexicon compiled by Amarasimha.
2. Aryabhatiya was composed by Aryabhata.
3. Brihatsamhita, an encyclopaedic work, was composed by Varahamihira.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 1 and 3 only  _(error: rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 2 only  _(error: rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **C.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 2, 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1, 2 and 3 ✅

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: Amarasimha is traditionally placed among the 'nine gems'
2. Statement 2 is correct: the astronomical-mathematical treatise of 499 CE
3. Statement 3 is correct: covers astronomy, astrology, architecture and more
4. Hence the answer is: 1, 2 and 3.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** All three are genuine Gupta-age attributions; do not assume one must be false.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-024 · L3 · hard · Freedom-movement leaders and their offices ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): Subhas Chandra Bose completed his full term as Congress president after the Tripuri session of 1939.

Reason (R): After his re-election at Tripuri over Gandhi-backed Pattabhi Sitaramayya, he could not secure a Working Committee acceptable to both sides.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A  _(error: treats R as the explanation of A)_
- **B.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **C.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **D.** A is false, but R is true ✅

**Working**

1. A is false: the Working Committee deadlock led Bose to resign in April 1939; Rajendra Prasad took over.
2. R is true — and it is the reason he resigned rather than completed the term.
3. A is false; R is true.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** The resignation followed the Working Committee impasse, not a defeat in election.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

| C. Chittaranjan Das | 3. Home Rule League (founded April 1916 at the Belgaum conference; HQ Poona) |
| D. B. R. Ambedkar | 4. Independent Labour Party |

Select the correct answer using the codes below:

- **A.** A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  _(error: mismatches A, C)_
- **B.** A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  _(error: mismatches A, C, D)_
- **C.** A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4 ✅
- **D.** A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3  _(error: mismatches A, B, D)_

**Working**

1. Bal Gangadhar Tilak → Home Rule League (founded April 1916 at the Belgaum conference; HQ Poona)
2. Gopal Krishna Gokhale → Servants of India Society
3. Chittaranjan Das → Swaraj Party
4. B. R. Ambedkar → Independent Labour Party
5. Code: A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Tilak and Annie Besant set up separate Home Rule Leagues in 1916; Gokhale founded the Servants of India Society (1905).

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-081 · L3 · hard · Civilian awards and the honours system ⚠ · officer

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 3 only ✅
- **C.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 and 2 only  _(error: accepts 2; rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: the number is restricted to a maximum of three per year
2. Statement 2 is incorrect: it is not mandatory to award it every year; many years have had none

4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 3 only.

**Working**

1. Art. 18 abolishes titles; the Court held national awards are recognitions of merit, not titles, subject to the no-prefix/suffix condition.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Misuse as a prefix/suffix can lead to forfeiture — that is the condition the Court imposed.

## GKA-104 · L3 · hard · Comparative government and world legislatures ⚠ · officer

Consider the following pairs (feature of the Indian Constitution — source):

1. Judicial review — United States
2. Nomination of members to the Rajya Sabha — Ireland
3. Joint sitting of the two Houses — Australia
4. Single citizenship — United States

How many of the pairs given above are correctly matched?

- **A.** Only one  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **B.** All four  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **C.** Only two  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **D.** Only three ✅

**Working**

1. Pair 1 is correct: borrowed from the US
2. Pair 2 is correct: borrowed from the Irish Constitution
3. Pair 3 is correct: borrowed from Australia
4. Pair 4 is incorrect: single citizenship is from the UK; the US has dual (federal and state) citizenship
5. 3 of 4 are correct — Only three.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** The US model is dual citizenship — the opposite of India's.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-105 · L2 · medium · Comparative government and world legislatures ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about the French Fifth Republic:

1. It has both a directly elected President and a Prime Minister.
2. Its Parliament consists of the National Assembly and the Senate.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** Both 1 and 2 ✅
- **B.** Neither 1 nor 2  _(error: mis-judges one statement)_
- **C.** 1 only  _(error: mis-judges one statement)_
- **D.** 2 only  _(error: mis-judges one statement)_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: semi-presidential system
2. Statement 2 is correct: bicameral
3. Hence the answer is: Both 1 and 2.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Both statements are correct.

1. Art. 17 abolishes untouchability and forbids its practice in any form.

## GKA-107 · L1 · easy · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · foundation

The Right to Constitutional Remedies is guaranteed under:

- **A.** Article 19  _(error: six freedoms)_
- **B.** Article 21  _(error: right to life)_
- **C.** Article 226  _(error: writ jurisdiction of High Courts — not a Fundamental Right)_
- **D.** Article 32 ✅

**Working**

1. Art. 32 — called the 'heart and soul' of the Constitution by Ambedkar — lets a person move the Supreme Court for enforcement of Fundamental Rights.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Art. 226 is wider but is not itself in Part III.

- **C.** 73rd Amendment Act, 1992  _(error: Panchayati Raj)_
- **D.** 86th Amendment Act, 2002 ✅

1. The 86th Amendment inserted Art. 21A, amended Art. 45 and added a Fundamental Duty (Art. 51A(k)).

## GKA-109 · L1 · easy · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · foundation

- **A.** 24th Amendment Act, 1971  _(error: affirmed Parliament's power to amend FRs)_

- **D.** 44th Amendment Act, 1978 ✅

1. The 44th Amendment deleted Arts. 19(1)(f) and 31 and inserted Art. 300A.

## GKA-110 · L1 · easy · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · foundation

- **D.** Certiorari  _(error: quashes an order of a lower court)_

**Working**

1. Habeas corpus ('to have the body') secures release from unlawful detention.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference

## GKA-111 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements:

1. The rights under Article 15 are available only to citizens.
2. The right under Article 21 is available only to citizens.
3. The rights under Article 30 are available only to citizens.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 2 only  _(error: accepts 2; rejects 1, 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 3 only ✅
- **C.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: Arts. 15, 16, 19, 29 and 30 are citizen-only rights
2. Statement 2 is incorrect: Art. 21 applies to all persons, citizens and foreigners
3. Statement 3 is correct: the minority educational rights are classed as citizen-only
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 3 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Life and liberty (Art. 21) extend to every person.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-112 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): The rights under Articles 20 and 21 cannot be suspended even during a National Emergency.

Reason (R): The 44th Amendment provided that the President cannot suspend the right to move courts for enforcement of Articles 20 and 21 under Article 359.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **B.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **C.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A ✅
- **D.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_

**Working**

1. After the 1975–77 experience, the 44th Amendment carved Arts. 20 and 21 out of Art. 359 suspension orders.
2. A is true; R is true; R does explain A.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Art. 19 is automatically suspended under Art. 358 in a war/external aggression emergency — not so Arts. 20 and 21.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

Match List I (Writ) with List II (Literal meaning):

| List I | List II |
|---|---|
| A. Mandamus | 1. To have the body |
| B. Quo warranto | 2. We command |
| C. Certiorari | 3. By what authority or warrant |
| D. Habeas corpus | 4. To be certified or informed |

Select the correct answer using the codes below:

- **A.** A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  _(error: mismatches B, C, D)_
- **B.** A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1  _(error: mismatches A, B, C)_
- **C.** A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  _(error: mismatches A, C, D)_
- **D.** A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1 ✅

**Working**

1. Mandamus → We command
2. Quo warranto → By what authority or warrant
3. Certiorari → To be certified or informed
4. Habeas corpus → To have the body
5. Code: A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Certiorari (to be informed) is often confused with prohibition.

- **D.** A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  _(error: mismatches A, C, D)_

**Working**

1. Article 22 → Protection against arrest and detention in certain cases
2. Article 23 → Prohibition of traffic in human beings and forced labour
3. Article 24 → Prohibition of employment of children in factories
4. Article 28 → Freedom from religious instruction in certain institutions
5. Code: A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2.

## GKA-115 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following protections and state how many are provided under Article 20:

1. No ex post facto criminal law increasing punishment
2. Protection against double jeopardy
3. Protection against self-incrimination for an accused
4. Protection against preventive detention without review

How many of the statements given above are correct?

- **A.** All four  _(error: miscounts the correct statements)_
- **B.** Only three ✅
- **C.** Only one  _(error: miscounts the correct statements)_
- **D.** Only two  _(error: miscounts the correct statements)_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: Art. 20(1)
2. Statement 2 is correct: Art. 20(2)
3. Statement 3 is correct: Art. 20(3)
4. Statement 4 is incorrect: that is Art. 22
5. 3 of 4 are correct — Only three.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference

## GKA-116 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Arrange the following Supreme Court judgments in chronological order:

1. Maneka Gandhi v. Union of India
2. I. C. Golaknath v. State of Punjab
3. A. K. Gopalan v. State of Madras
4. Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala

Select the correct chronological order (earliest first):

- **A.** 3 – 1 – 4 – 2  _(error: misplaces 2, 1 in the sequence)_
- **B.** 4 – 3 – 2 – 1  _(error: misplaces 3, 2, 4 in the sequence)_
- **C.** 3 – 2 – 4 – 1 ✅
- **D.** 4 – 2 – 3 – 1  _(error: misplaces 3, 4 in the sequence)_

**Working**

1. A. K. Gopalan v. State of Madras — 1950
2. I. C. Golaknath v. State of Punjab — 1967
3. Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala — 1973
4. Maneka Gandhi v. Union of India — 1978
5. Order: 3 – 2 – 4 – 1.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Maneka Gandhi (1978) overruled the narrow Gopalan reading of Art. 21.

## GKA-117 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): The right to privacy is a Fundamental Right.

Reason (R): In Justice K. S. Puttaswamy v. Union of India (2017), the Supreme Court held that privacy is protected only under Article 19.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **B.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **C.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A  _(error: treats R as the explanation of A)_
- **D.** A is true, but R is false ✅

**Working**

1. A is true.
2. R is false: the nine-judge bench held privacy intrinsic to Article 21 and to the freedoms of Part III as a whole, not Article 19 alone.
3. A is true; R is false.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Earlier cases (M. P. Sharma, Kharak Singh) had denied it; Puttaswamy overruled them.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-118 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about Article 19:

1. Freedom of the press is expressly mentioned in Article 19.
2. Article 19 originally guaranteed seven freedoms.
3. The right to form cooperative societies was added to Article 19(1)(c) by the 97th Amendment.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 1 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 1; rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **C.** 2 and 3 only ✅
- **D.** 3 only  _(error: rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is incorrect: it is implied in freedom of speech and expression, Art. 19(1)(a)
2. Statement 2 is correct: 19(1)(f) property was deleted in 1978, leaving six
3. Statement 3 is correct: 97th Amendment Act, 2011
4. Hence the answer is: 2 and 3 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Press freedom is judicially derived, not expressly stated.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## GKA-119 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about writs:

1. The writ jurisdiction of the Supreme Court under Article 32 is wider than that of High Courts under Article 226.
2. A writ of quo warranto can be sought by any interested person, not only an aggrieved one.
3. Mandamus cannot be issued against the President or a state Governor acting in official capacity.

Which of the statements given above is/are NOT correct?

- **A.** 1 and 2 only  _(error: accepts 2 (correct statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 only ✅
- **C.** 2 only  _(error: accepts 2; rejects 1 (correct statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 3 (correct statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is incorrect: HCs can issue writs for FRs and 'for any other purpose', so their writ jurisdiction is wider
2. Statement 2 is correct: relaxed locus standi
3. Statement 3 is correct: they are protected under Art. 361
4. Hence the answer is: 1 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Art. 32 is itself a Fundamental Right, but its scope is narrower than Art. 226.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## GKA-120 · L3 · hard · Fundamental Rights ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): The right to property is no longer a Fundamental Right.

Reason (R): It is now a constitutional right under Article 300A.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A ✅
- **B.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **C.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **D.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A  _(error: treats R as the explanation of A)_

**Working**

1. A is true because the 44th Amendment (1978) deleted Arts. 19(1)(f) and 31.
2. R states its present status, but the cause of A is the 44th Amendment, not Art. 300A itself.
3. A is true; R is true; R does not explain A.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** A description of the current position is not the reason for the change.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-127 · L3 · hard · Judiciary and judicial administration ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about the removal of a Supreme Court judge:

1. A judge can be removed by the President on an address by Parliament passed by a special majority in each House.
2. The grounds are proved misbehaviour or incapacity.
3. The motion must be passed at a joint sitting of both Houses.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 2 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 3; rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 2 only ✅
- **C.** 2 only  _(error: rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: Art. 124(4)
2. Statement 2 is correct: Art. 124(4)
3. Statement 3 is incorrect: each House must pass it separately; there is no joint sitting
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 2 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Joint sittings apply only to ordinary bills (Art. 108).

| List I | List II |
|---|---|
| A. Article 127 | 1. Appointment of district judges |
| B. Article 128 | 2. Appointment of ad hoc judges in the Supreme Court |
| C. Article 224 | 3. Attendance of retired judges at sittings of the Supreme Court |
| D. Article 233 | 4. Additional and acting judges of High Courts |

Select the correct answer using the codes below:

- **A.** A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  _(error: mismatches A, C, D)_
- **B.** A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  _(error: mismatches A, B, D)_
- **C.** A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1 ✅
- **D.** A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  _(error: mismatches B, C, D)_

**Working**

1. Article 127 → Appointment of ad hoc judges in the Supreme Court
2. Article 128 → Attendance of retired judges at sittings of the Supreme Court
3. Article 224 → Additional and acting judges of High Courts
4. Article 233 → Appointment of district judges
5. Code: A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Art. 224A (retired HC judges) is distinct from Art. 224 (additional judges).

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

Choose the correct option:

- **B.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **C.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **D.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A ✅

**Working**

1. Both statements are true.
2. The binding effect of SC law comes from Art. 141, not Art. 142.
3. A is true; R is true; R does not explain A.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

- **C.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 and 2 only  _(error: accepts 2; rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: only elected members of Parliament are in the electoral college
2. Statement 2 is incorrect: only elected members of Legislative Assemblies vote
3. Statement 3 is correct: added by the 70th Amendment, 1992
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 3 only.

## GKA-142 · L3 · hard · President and Vice-President of India ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about the Vice-President:

2. A resolution for removal of the Vice-President can be moved only in the Rajya Sabha.
3. The Vice-President can be removed only by impeachment under the procedure applicable to the President.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 2 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 3; rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 1 and 2 only ✅
- **C.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: the electoral college is all members of both Houses
2. Statement 2 is correct: Art. 67(b)
3. Statement 3 is incorrect: removal is by an RS resolution passed by an effective majority and agreed to by the LS — not impeachment
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 2 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** The VP's removal is simpler than the President's impeachment.

## GKA-146 · L3 · hard · President and Vice-President of India ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about the pardoning power:

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 1 and 2 only ✅
- **B.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

2. Statement 2 is correct: Art. 72(1)(b)
3. Statement 3 is incorrect: courts may review it on limited grounds such as mala fides or arbitrariness
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 2 only.

| B. Suspensive veto | 2. Returning a bill for reconsideration |
| C. Pocket veto | 3. Withholding assent to a bill |

## GKA-158 · L3 · hard · Recent central legislation and statutory penalties ⚠ · officer

Consider the following statements about the Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023:

1. It establishes the Data Protection Board of India.
2. The individual to whom personal data relates is called the 'Data Principal'.
3. The penalty for a single category of breach can extend up to ₹250 crore.
4. Appeals against the Board's orders lie directly to the Supreme Court.

How many of the statements given above are correct?

- **A.** All four  _(error: miscounts the correct statements)_
- **B.** Only two  _(error: miscounts the correct statements)_
- **C.** Only three ✅
- **D.** Only one  _(error: miscounts the correct statements)_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: adjudicatory body under the Act
2. Statement 2 is correct: the processor-controller is the 'Data Fiduciary'
3. Statement 3 is correct: maximum in the Schedule for failure of security safeguards
4. Statement 4 is incorrect: appeals lie to the Telecom Disputes Settlement and Appellate Tribunal (TDSAT)
5. 3 of 4 are correct — Only three.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** TDSAT is the appellate forum, not the Supreme Court.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-173 · L3 · hard · Schedules of the Constitution ⚠ · officer

- **A.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 3 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 2 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 2; rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **C.** 1 and 3 only ✅
- **D.** 3 only  _(error: rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

2. Statement 2 is incorrect: Nagaland has Art. 371A; Manipur's hill areas are under Art. 371C, not the Sixth Schedule

4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 3 only.

**Trap:** Only four north-eastern states are covered.

## GKA-174 · L3 · hard · Schedules of the Constitution ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): Laws placed in the Ninth Schedule after 24 April 1973 can be challenged if they violate the basic structure.

Reason (R): The Supreme Court so held in Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala (1973).

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A  _(error: treats R as the explanation of A)_
- **B.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **C.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **D.** A is true, but R is false ✅

**Working**

1. A is true.
2. R is false: the holding is from I. R. Coelho (2007); 24 April 1973 is only the date of the Kesavananda judgment used as the cut-off.
3. A is true; R is false.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** 24 April 1973 is the date of the Kesavananda Bharati judgment.

## GKA-186 · L3 · hard · Statutory commissions and information oversight ⚠ · officer

- **B.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **C.** 1 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 1; rejects 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

2. 'Mother India' (1927) was written by Sarojini Naidu.
3. 'Unhappy India' was written by Lala Lajpat Rai.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

- **A.** 3 only  _(error: rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **B.** 2 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 2; rejects 1 (incorrect statements misjudged))_
- **C.** 1 and 3 only ✅
- **D.** 1, 2 and 3  _(error: accepts 2 (incorrect statements misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Statement 1 is correct: yes
2. Statement 2 is incorrect: it was written by the American author Katherine Mayo
3. Statement 3 is correct: as a rebuttal to Mayo
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 3 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Unhappy India was a reply to Mother India.

## GKA-338 · L3 · hard · Business and startup terminology ⚠ · officer

A startup raises ₹30 crore from an investor at a pre-money valuation of ₹120 crore. What percentage stake does the investor receive?

- **A.** 20% ✅
- **B.** 25%  _(error: divided investment by pre-money instead of post-money valuation)_
- **C.** 80%  _(error: reported founders' residual stake)_
- **D.** 30%  _(error: read the investment amount as a percentage)_

**Working**

1. Post-money valuation = 120 + 30 = ₹150 crore
2. Investor stake = 30 ÷ 150 = 20%

**Formula:** Investor stake = Investment ÷ Post-money valuation  
**Trap:** Use post-money, not pre-money, as the base.

## GKA-343 · L3 · hard · Business and startup terminology ⚠ · officer

Assertion (A): EBITDA is used to compare the operating performance of firms with different capital structures.

Reason (R): EBITDA includes interest expense so as to reflect each firm's financing costs.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** A is true, but R is false ✅
- **B.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **C.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **D.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A  _(error: treats R as the explanation of A)_

**Working**

1. A is true.
2. R is false: EBITDA is earnings BEFORE interest, taxes, depreciation and amortisation — interest is excluded, which is what makes cross-capital-structure comparison possible.
3. A is true; R is false.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Read the acronym: 'before interest'.

## GKA-344 · L3 · hard · Business and startup terminology ⚠ · officer

An employee is granted 4,800 stock options vesting in equal monthly instalments over 48 months, with a 12-month cliff (nothing vests before month 12; at month 12 the first 12 months' options vest together). If the employee leaves after 30 months, how many options have vested?

- **A.** 3,600  _(error: rounded up to three years)_
- **B.** 3,000 ✅
- **C.** 2,400  _(error: counted only completed years (2 years))_
- **D.** 1,200  _(error: counted only the cliff tranche)_

**Working**

1. Monthly vesting = 4,800 ÷ 48 = 100
2. Cliff passed (30 ≥ 12), so vested = 100 × 30 = 3,000

**Formula:** Vested = (Grant ÷ Vesting months) × Months served, if months ≥ cliff  
**Trap:** The cliff only delays vesting; it does not reset it.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GKA-415 · L3 · hard · World currencies ⚠ · officer

Match List I (ISO 4217 code) with List II (Currency):

| List I | List II |
|---|---|
| A. CHF | 1. South African rand |
| B. ZAR | 2. Swiss franc |
| C. CNY | 3. Brazilian real |
| D. BRL | 4. Chinese yuan renminbi |

Select the correct answer using the codes below:

- **A.** A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  _(error: mismatches A, D)_
- **B.** A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3  _(error: mismatches A, C)_
- **C.** A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3 ✅
- **D.** A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  _(error: mismatches A, C, D)_

**Working**

1. CHF → Swiss franc
2. ZAR → South African rand
3. CNY → Chinese yuan renminbi
4. BRL → Brazilian real
5. Code: A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** CHF comes from the Latin 'Confoederatio Helvetica'.

## GKA-416 · L3 · hard · World currencies ⚠ · officer

Consider the following pairs (country — currency):

1. Ukraine — Hryvnia
2. Kazakhstan — Tenge
3. Hungary — Koruna
4. Georgia — Dram

How many of the pairs given above are correctly matched?

- **A.** Only two ✅
- **B.** Only one  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **C.** All four  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_
- **D.** Only three  _(error: miscounts the correct pairs)_

**Working**

1. Pair 1 is correct: yes
2. Pair 2 is correct: yes
3. Pair 3 is incorrect: Hungary uses the forint; koruna is Czech
4. Pair 4 is incorrect: Georgia uses the lari; dram is Armenian
5. 2 of 4 are correct — Only two.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Neighbouring countries' currencies are the traps.

## GKA-417 · L3 · hard · World currencies ⚠ · officer

Consider the following:

Assertion (A): The Bhutanese ngultrum and the Indian rupee exchange at par.

Reason (R): The ngultrum is pegged to the Indian rupee at 1:1.

Choose the correct option:

- **A.** A is true, but R is false  _(error: wrongly rejects R)_
- **B.** A is false, but R is true  _(error: wrongly rejects A)_
- **C.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_
- **D.** Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A ✅

**Working**

1. The fixed 1:1 peg means they exchange at par.
2. A is true; R is true; R does explain A.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** R explains A.

## GKA-418 · L3 · hard · World currencies ⚠ · officer

Consider the following pairs (country — currency):

1. United Arab Emirates — Dirham
2. Kuwait — Dinar
3. Saudi Arabia — Dinar

Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly matched?

- **A.** 1 and 2 only ✅
- **B.** 2 only  _(error: rejects 1 (incorrect pairs misjudged))_
- **C.** 1 only  _(error: rejects 2 (incorrect pairs misjudged))_
- **D.** 1 and 3 only  _(error: accepts 3; rejects 2 (incorrect pairs misjudged))_

**Working**

1. Pair 1 is correct: yes
2. Pair 2 is correct: yes
3. Pair 3 is incorrect: Saudi Arabia uses the riyal
4. Hence the answer is: 1 and 2 only.

**Formula:** Static fact (durable); verify against reference  
**Trap:** Riyal: Saudi Arabia and Qatar; dinar: Kuwait, Bahrain, Iraq, Jordan.

- **D.** Both A and R are true, but R is not the correct explanation of A  _(error: misses the causal link between R and A)_

**Working**

1. The distinction in R explains the dual usage.

**Working**

1. Sweden → Krona

5. Code: A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2.
